Papers: **D** = "On the Functional Dimension of a Family of Analytic Neural Networks",
**S** = "Skip Connections Unlock at Least d_model² Parameters per Layer",
**A** = "Can an MLP Absorb Its Skip Connection Exactly?". File names are relative to
`lean/SkipsLean/`.

## The list of numbered statements

`numbered_statements.json` was compared with every heading "Theorem / Proposition / Lemma /
Corollary N (title)" of the three texts. It agrees: D 34 (7 main, 27 appendix), S 35 (10, 25),
A 30 (13, 17). No statement is missing from the list and none is wrongly in it. The numbers that
the list skips are definitions, remarks and examples (D 3.1, 4.1, 4.2, 4.4, 4.5, A.2, B.2, C.4 to
C.7, D.21; S 3.1, 4.1, 4.3, 4.4, A.6, A.7, A.10, A.11, A.13, A.16, A.19, A.20, A.22, A.25, A.29,
A.31, A.34, B.4, B.5; A 5.2, 5.3, A.8, A.12, A.16, C.3 to C.5, C.7).

## How a status was decided

- `proved`: every clause and every case, as the paper words them (parts (i), (ii), …; kinds of
  block; without normalization, `ε > 0`, `ε = 0`), follows from Lean theorems that are proved. A
  Lean statement that is stronger or differs only in form counts, and the note says so.
- `in part`: some clause or worded case is proved and some is not; or Lean proves only a
  restricted version that the library gives as its version of this statement (a real variable
  for a complex one, the computations of a proposition about networks). `in part+`: some of the
  missing part is stated in Lean and in work.
- `in work`: Lean statements exist, none of the paper's clauses is proved yet. Proved steps of the
  proof do not make a clause proved; the note names them.
- `not started`: no Lean statement of the statement, of a clause or of a worded case. If only an
  instance is proved that is another numbered statement (one block for a theorem on `L` blocks),
  the status stays `not started` and the note says it.
- A statement is judged by every Lean theorem that covers it, also one written for another paper.
- Files in work at `1d88b3d` (statements with `sorry`): `Depth/RayInduction.lean`,
  `Depth/FrozenFar.lean`, `Depth/RayGatedPlus.lean`, `Depth/RayMlpPlus.lean`,
  `Symmetry/OnlyOrthogonal.lean`. `Depth/RayGatedN.lean`, `Depth/StepGated.lean`,
  `Depth/GeluGatedExplicit.lean` and `Depth/GeluGatedTheorem.lean` have no `sorry` of their own
  and rest on these, so they count as in work. All other files count as proved; among them
  `Depth/StepsPlus.lean`, which the brief for this table still listed as in work.
- No attention block, no bias and no LayerNorm is defined in Lean. Every statement or case about
  them is missing for this reason.

## Cases where I was unsure

1. **D Lemma D.13: `in part`, could be `proved`.** Lean proves that good parameters exist. If an
   existence statement stands for the whole lemma, it is `proved`. I chose `in part` because the
   second sentence of the lemma (`A_ℓ = {1, …, d}`, `g°_ℓ(n) = n`, `(n𝟙) n`) has no Lean statement.
   The fifth audit calls the Lean form weaker; it is enough for the use in Appendix D.7.
2. **A Theorem 4.1 and A Corollary A.3: `proved`, could be `in part`.** For the class "positively
   homogeneous of degree `k > 1`" Lean assumes in addition that the fixed map is bounded on the
   unit sphere (a continuous map is). The paper does not state this and its proof uses it
   ("`O(‖x‖^k)`"). ReLU², ReGLU and all gated blocks are proved with no added hypothesis. No Lean
   statement shows the bound for a general homogeneous activation.
3. **D Proposition 3.3: `proved`, could be `in part`.** The sentence "these changes of basis at
   all `L+1` hidden states remove `(L+1) dim 𝒢` parameters" has no Lean statement of its own. Lean
   proves the reductions at all hidden states together and counts the entries below a diagonal;
   the product with `L+1` is not written. The same holds for the counts in S A.9 (i) and S A.14 (i).
4. **D Lemma C.2: `proved`.** The dimension `2d²` is not a statement of its own; it follows in one
   line from `cobDir_injective` and `finrank_cobDomain`, and the Lean proofs use it in this way.
5. **D Proposition A.3 and S Proposition A.15: `in part`, could be `not started`.** No clause of
   the proposition is a Lean statement; only computations of its proof are (for (ii), the identity
   for one row). The first audit calls this "part (computations of the proof only)".
6. **D Lemma B.1 and S Lemma A.18: `in part`.** I counted the infinitesimal form (the directions
   determine the matrices) as the lemma for MLP blocks, since the two forms are equivalent for a
   Lie group action; this equivalence is not in Lean. If the infinitesimal form does not count,
   no clause of these lemmas is proved.
7. **S Theorem 4.11 and S Theorem A.28: `in part`, where the index says "not stated".** No Lean
   statement was written for them, but the clause "equals no skipless network of the same widths"
   follows, for MLP blocks under pre-RMSNorm, from the Lean theorems for A Theorem 4.3, and without
   normalization for tanh, SwiGLU and GeGLU from those for A Theorem 4.1 (there for every `θ` with
   `ED ≠ 0`; that this holds at generic parameters is not a Lean statement). If only statements
   written for the skips paper count, both are `not started`.
8. **S Theorem 4.7 and S Corollary 4.8: `not started`, could be `in part`.** Their instance `L = 1`
   without normalization is proved, as Theorem 4.6 (i). I did not count an instance that is
   another numbered statement. Nothing is stated for `L ≥ 2` or under pre-RMSNorm.
9. **D Lemmas D.8, D.9, D.10: `in part`.** Lean has them for a real variable only (D.10 also
   without the derivatives of higher order). This is a restricted version, not one of the paper's
   clauses; it is what the cases (n) and (+) use. The complex versions serve the case (0) and
   Lemma D.11.
10. **D Theorems 5.1, C.1 and S Theorems 4.6, B.1, the kinds ReLU, ReLU², ReGLU: counted as
    proved.** The Lean `funDim` is not the paper's functional dimension by definition for blocks
    with a kink (it is at least the paper's value). For one block the paper's statement is the
    pair "value of `funDim`" and "a batch off the kinks with that rank"; the fourth audit checked
    that the pair gives the paper's statement.
11. **"Equality condition".** Definitions D 4.4 and S 4.3 are not in Lean. Where a theorem says
    "so the network satisfies the equality condition", I took the stated equality at generic
    parameters as that clause.
12. **S Theorem 3.3 (i).** The Lean "only if" has the hypothesis of Theorem A.5 (iii) (every block
    is nonzero for some value of its parameters), which the main text leaves to its scope. No Lean
    statement shows it for the named kinds of block. The theorem is `in part` in any case, because
    of (ii).
13. I did not compile anything. The statuses of the Lean files are those of the index, of the
    coordinator's note on the files proved since, and of a search for `sorry` in the sources at
    `1d88b3d` (found only in the five files named above). The branch moved once while I read; if
    it has moved again, the rows for Appendix D of the dimension paper and for
    `Symmetry/OnlyOrthogonal.lean` are the ones to look at.
